323→        return None
324→    opening, debit_amt, credit_amt, closing = (balances or [None, None, None, None])[:4]
325→    debit_count, credit_count = (counts or [None, None])[:2]
326→    return StatementSummary(
327→        opening_balance=opening,
328→        closing_balance=closing,
329→        debit_count=debit_count,
330→        credit_count=credit_count,
331→        debit_amount=debit_amt,
332→        credit_amount=credit_amt,
333→    )
334→
335→
336→def parse_summary(pdf_path: Path | str, password: str = "") -> StatementSummary | None:
337→    """Read the bank's own SUMMARY figures from the statement, or None if absent."""
338→    with pdfplumber.open(str(Path(pdf_path)), password=password or "") as pdf:
339→        for page in pdf.pages:
340→            text = page.extract_text() or ""
341→            if "SUMMARY" in text:
342→                summary = _parse_summary_lines(text.splitlines())
343→                if summary is not None:
344→                    return summary
345→    return None
346→
347→
348→def validate_against_summary(
349→    statement: Statement, summary: StatementSummary, tol: float = 0.01
350→) -> ValidationReport:
351→    """Cross-check a parse against the bank's SUMMARY: reconciliation + bookends.
352→
353→    Maps HDFC's rows onto the canonical concepts and delegates to the shared verifier
354→    (:func:`muneem.parsers.validation.verify`): reconciliation catches any mid-statement
355→    error/omission, the Dr/Cr counts catch any extra/missing row, and the opening & closing
356→    balances catch a missing first/last row.
357→    """
358→    rows = [t.to_concept() for t in statement.transactions]
359→    return verify(rows, summary, tol)
360→
361→
362→def write_csv(statement: Statement, out_path: Path | str) -> Path:
363→    """Write parsed transactions to CSV for local QA (the 'export to a sheet' idea).
364→
365→    Writes real data — call only for a local, gitignored destination.
366→    """
367→    out = Path(out_path)
368→    out.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
369→    with open(out, "w", newline="", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
370→        writer = csv.writer(fh)
371→        writer.writerow(
372→            [
373→                "date",
374→                "value_date",
375→                "narration",
376→                "ref",
377→                "withdrawal",
378→                "deposit",
379→                "closing_balance",
380→                "page",
381→            ]
382→        )
383→        for t in statement.transactions:
384→            writer.writerow(
385→                [
386→                    t.date,
387→                    t.value_date or "",
388→                    t.narration,
389→                    t.ref or "",
390→                    f"{t.withdrawal:.2f}",
391→                    f"{t.deposit:.2f}",
392→                    f"{t.closing_balance:.2f}",
393→                    t.page,
394→                ]
395→            )
396→    return out
397→
398→
399→@register_parser
400→class HDFCParser(StatementParser):
401→    """Registered parser for HDFC savings / transaction-account statements."""
402→
403→    def can_handle(self, text: str, sender: str | None = None, subject: str | None = None) -> bool:
404→        low = (text or "").lower()
405→        is_hdfc = "hdfc bank" in low or "hdfcbank" in low or "hdfcbank" in (sender or "").lower()
406→        is_statement = "savings account details" in low or (
407→            "narration" in low and "closing balance" in low
408→        )
409→        return is_hdfc and is_statement
410→
411→    def parse_and_insert(
412→        self,
413→        pdf_path: Path,
414→        conn,
415→        doc_id: int,
416→        password: str | None = None,
417→    ) -> int:
418→        """Parse, insert rows into ``hdfc_transactions``, and record parse quality.
419→
420→        Sets ``documents.status`` to 'reconciled' / 'unreconciled' based on the
421→        balance walk (and the bank's SUMMARY when present) so a downstream consumer
422→        can tell a trustworthy parse from a suspect one. Returns the row count.
423→        """
424→        stmt = parse_statement(pdf_path, password=password or "")
425→        for t in stmt.transactions:
426→            dbmod.insert_hdfc_transaction(
427→                conn,
428→                document_id=doc_id,
429→                account_number=stmt.account_number,
430→                txn_date=t.date,
431→                value_date=t.value_date,
432→                particulars=t.narration,
433→                ref=t.ref,
434→                withdrawal=t.withdrawal,
435→                deposit=t.deposit,
436→                closing_balance=t.closing_balance,
437→            )
438→        summary = parse_summary(pdf_path, password=password or "")
439→        ok = (
440→            validate_against_summary(stmt, summary).ok
441→            if summary is not None
442→            else reconcile(stmt).ok
443→        )
444→        verdict = Confidence.RECONCILED if ok else Confidence.UNRECONCILED
445→        conn.execute(
446→            "UPDATE documents SET status = ? WHERE id = ?",
447→            (verdict.value, doc_id),
448→        )
449→        return len(stmt.transactions)
450→

### 41. tool_result

1→"""Axis Bank statement parsers: savings/transaction account + credit card.
2→
3→**Savings runs on the shared engine-driven path** (:mod:`muneem.parsers.savings`): per-table +
4→coordinate-clustered candidates → engine mapping → reconciliation oracle, **oracle-gated** so a
5→vintage it can't read stores nothing (``unreconciled``) rather than mislabeled rows. Axis specifics
6→live entirely in ``AXIS_PROFILE``; this replaced the old fixed-column ``extract_tables`` mapping.
7→
8→Credit-card statements carry no running balance, so ``AxisCreditCardParser`` can't reconcile
9→(parse/persist split + count only). No statement content is logged here.
10→"""
11→
12→from __future__ import annotations
13→
14→import re
15→import sqlite3
16→from dataclasses import dataclass
17→from pathlib import Path
18→
19→import pdfplumber
20→
21→from muneem import db as dbmod
22→
23→from . import register_parser
24→from .base import StatementParser
25→from .profiles import AXIS_PROFILE
26→from .savings import SavingsParse, parse_savings, parse_statement_summary, txn_line_predicate
27→from .validation import Confidence, parse_amount
28→
29→_CC_DATE_RE = re.compile(r"\d{2}/\d{2}/\d{4}")  # Axis credit card: dd/mm/yyyy
30→_CARD_RES = [re.compile(r"(\d{4}XXXX\d{4})"), re.compile(r"(\d{6}\*+\d{4})")]
31→
32→
33→def _first_match(text: str, patterns: list[re.Pattern]) -> str | None:
34→    for rx in patterns:
35→        m = rx.search(text)
36→        if m:
37→            return m.group(1).strip()
38→    return None
39→
40→
41→# --- Savings / transaction account (concept core) ---------------------------------------------
42→
43→
44→# Back-compat names; the savings logic now lives in muneem.parsers.savings (shared with AU Bank).
45→AxisSavings = SavingsParse
46→parse_axis_summary = parse_statement_summary
47→_is_txn_line = txn_line_predicate(AXIS_PROFILE)
48→
49→
50→def parse_axis_savings(pdf_path: Path, password: str | None = None) -> SavingsParse:
51→    """Parse an Axis savings statement via the shared engine-driven path (savings.parse_savings)."""
52→    return parse_savings(pdf_path, AXIS_PROFILE, password)
53→
54→
55→@register_parser
56→class AxisParser(StatementParser):
57→    def can_handle(self, text: str, sender: str | None = None, subject: str | None = None) -> bool:
58→        if sender and "axisbank.com" in sender.lower():
59→            return True
60→        t = text or ""
61→        return "Axis Bank" in t and ("Statement of Account" in t or "Account Statement" in t)
62→
63→    def parse_and_insert(
64→        self, pdf_path: Path, conn: sqlite3.Connection, doc_id: int, password: str | None = None
65→    ) -> int:
66→        """Persist a savings parse **only if it reconciles**; otherwise store nothing.
67→
68→        Sets ``documents.status`` to ``reconciled`` / ``unreconciled`` accordingly, so a layout the
69→        parser can't yet read surfaces loudly instead of saving mislabeled rows.
70→        """
71→        try:
72→            parsed = parse_axis_savings(pdf_path, password)
73→        except Exception as exc:
74→            raise RuntimeError(f"Failed to parse Axis statement {pdf_path}: {exc}") from exc
75→
76→        if not parsed.verified:
77→            conn.execute(
78→                "UPDATE documents SET status = ? WHERE id = ?",
79→                (Confidence.UNRECONCILED.value, doc_id),
80→            )
81→            return 0
82→
83→        for r in parsed.rows:
84→            dbmod.insert_axis_transaction(
85→                conn,
86→                document_id=doc_id,
87→                account_number=parsed.account_number,
88→                txn_date=r.txn_date,
89→                value_date=r.value_date or "",
90→                particulars=r.narration,
91→                debit=r.debit,
92→                credit=r.credit,
93→                balance=r.balance,
94→            )
95→        conn.execute(
96→            "UPDATE documents SET status = ? WHERE id = ?",
97→            (Confidence.RECONCILED.value, doc_id),
98→        )
99→        return len(parsed.rows)
100→

### 43. tool_result

100→
101→
102→# --- Credit card (no running balance; count only) ---------------------------------------------
103→
104→
105→@dataclass
106→class AxisCCTxn:
107→    txn_date: str
108→    particulars: str
109→    merchant_category: str
110→    amount: float
111→    type: str  # "Cr" or "Dr"
112→
113→
114→def _rows_from_cc_tables(tables: list) -> list[AxisCCTxn]:
115→    """Build credit-card transactions from extracted tables. Pure: no PDF, no DB."""
116→    rows: list[AxisCCTxn] = []
117→    for table in tables or []:
118→        for row in table or []:
119→            if not row or len(row) < 9:
120→                continue
121→            date_str = str(row[0]).strip() if row[0] else ""
122→            if not _CC_DATE_RE.match(date_str):
123→                continue
124→            amount_str = str(row[8]).strip() if row[8] else ""
125→            is_cr = "Cr" in amount_str
126→            amount = parse_amount(re.sub(r"[^\d.]", "", amount_str)) or 0.0
127→            rows.append(
128→                AxisCCTxn(
129→                    txn_date=date_str,
130→                    particulars=str(row[2]).strip() if row[2] else "",
131→                    merchant_category=str(row[7]).strip() if row[7] else "",
132→                    amount=amount,
133→                    type="Cr" if is_cr else "Dr",
134→                )
135→            )
136→    return rows
137→
138→
139→def parse_axis_cc(pdf_path: Path, password: str | None = None) -> tuple[str, list[AxisCCTxn]]:
140→    """Open an Axis credit-card statement and return (card_number, transactions)."""
141→    card_number = "UNKNOWN"
142→    rows: list[AxisCCTxn] = []
143→    with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path, password=password) as pdf:
144→        head = "\n".join((pdf.pages[i].extract_text() or "") for i in range(min(3, len(pdf.pages))))
145→        card_number = _first_match(head, _CARD_RES) or "UNKNOWN"
146→        for page in pdf.pages:
147→            rows.extend(_rows_from_cc_tables(page.extract_tables()))
148→    return card_number, rows
149→
150→
151→@register_parser
152→class AxisCreditCardParser(StatementParser):
153→    def can_handle(self, text: str, sender: str | None = None, subject: str | None = None) -> bool:
154→        return "Axis Bank" in text and "Credit Card Statement" in text
155→
156→    def parse_and_insert(
157→        self, pdf_path: Path, conn: sqlite3.Connection, doc_id: int, password: str | None = None
158→    ) -> int:
159→        try:
160→            card_number, rows = parse_axis_cc(pdf_path, password)
161→        except Exception as exc:
162→            raise RuntimeError(f"Failed to parse Axis CC statement {pdf_path}: {exc}") from exc
163→        for t in rows:
164→            dbmod.insert_axis_cc_transaction(
165→                conn,
166→                document_id=doc_id,
167→                account_number=card_number,
168→                txn_date=t.txn_date,
169→                particulars=t.particulars,
170→                merchant_category=t.merchant_category,
171→                amount=t.amount,
172→                type=t.type,
173→            )
174→        # Credit-card statements carry no running balance, so reconciliation does not apply;
175→        # documents.status is left as set by the caller.
176→        return len(rows)
177→

### 44. tool_result

1→"""Shared engine-driven savings-statement parsing (Axis, AU Bank, and future issuers).
2→
3→Every issuer whose statement is a transaction table follows the same recipe, so it lives here once
4→and each bank supplies only a :class:`~muneem.parsers.engine.Profile`:
5→
6→1. **Extraction proposes** candidate cell-matrices — one per ``extract_tables()`` table, plus a
7→   coordinate-clustered whole-statement matrix (for layouts ``extract_tables`` mis-segments).
8→2. The generic **engine** maps each candidate's columns onto the canonical concepts.
9→3. **Reconciliation disposes:** a parse is trusted only if its per-row balance walks, or — when a
10→   vintage has no per-row balance — the totals reconcile to the printed ``opening``/``closing``.
11→
12→A statement splits its ledger across several extracted tables (page breaks), so we map each table
13→on its own, keep the rows the oracle endorses, and re-check the *combined* set (which catches a
14→cross-table balance break, i.e. a missing transaction). The result is **oracle-gated**: a layout we
15→can't yet read returns ``verified=False`` and the caller persists nothing — never mislabeled rows.
16→
17→No statement content is logged; only counts/booleans travel out.
18→"""
19→
20→from __future__ import annotations
21→
22→import re
23→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
24→from pathlib import Path
25→
26→import pdfplumber
27→
28→from .clustering import cluster_to_matrix
29→from .concepts import ConceptRow, StatementConcepts
30→from .engine import Profile, map_table
31→from .validation import parse_amount, reconcile_running_balance, reconcile_total
32→
33→_MONEY_TOKEN = re.compile(r"^-?[\d,]+\.\d{2}(?:cr|dr)?$", re.IGNORECASE)
34→_OPENING_RE = re.compile(r"opening\s+balance[^\d\-]*(-?[\d,]+\.\d{2})", re.IGNORECASE)
35→_CLOSING_RE = re.compile(r"closing\s+balance[^\d\-]*(-?[\d,]+\.\d{2})", re.IGNORECASE)
36→
37→
38→@dataclass
39→class SavingsParse:
40→    """Outcome of a savings parse: concept rows + whether reconciliation endorsed them."""
41→
42→    account_number: str
43→    rows: list[ConceptRow] = field(default_factory=list)
44→    verified: bool = False
45→
46→
47→def parse_statement_summary(text: str) -> StatementConcepts:
48→    """Read opening/closing balance from statement text (for the total-reconcile oracle).
49→
50→    Best-effort regex; ``None`` when not found — the oracle then relies on the per-row walk.
51→    """
52→    opening = _OPENING_RE.search(text)
53→    closing = _CLOSING_RE.search(text)
54→    return StatementConcepts(
55→        opening_balance=parse_amount(opening.group(1)) if opening else None,
56→        closing_balance=parse_amount(closing.group(1)) if closing else None,
57→    )
58→
59→
60→def txn_line_predicate(profile: Profile):
61→    """A line is transaction-like if it carries one of the profile's dates *and* a money token.
62→
63→    Used to restrict coordinate clustering to the transaction band (drops headers / summaries /
64→    address blocks that would otherwise wreck column inference).
65→    """
66→    patterns = profile.date_patterns
67→
68→    def keep(texts: list[str]) -> bool:
69→        has_date = any(any(p.match(t) for p in patterns) for t in texts)
70→        return has_date and any(_MONEY_TOKEN.match(t) for t in texts)
71→
72→    return keep
73→
74→
75→def reconcile_oracle(opening: float | None, closing: float | None):
76→    """Trust a parse if the per-row balance walks, else if the totals reconcile to the bookends."""
77→
78→    def check(rows: list[ConceptRow]) -> bool:
79→        if reconcile_running_balance([(r.debit, r.credit, r.balance) for r in rows]).ok:
80→            return True
81→        if rows and opening is not None and closing is not None:
82→            return reconcile_total(rows, opening, closing)
83→        return False
84→
85→    return check
86→
87→
88→def _reconstruct_by_balance_delta(
89→    matrix: list[list[str]], profile: Profile, opening: float | None, tol: float = 0.01
90→) -> list[ConceptRow]:
91→    """Recover debit/credit from the running balance when columns won't split cleanly.
92→
93→    Needs only three things per row — a date, the transaction amount, and the running balance — so
94→    it tolerates messy column inference (a sliver column, an interleaved value-date column, or a
95→    single merged withdrawal/deposit column). Direction is the sign of the balance change; the
96→    caller's reconciliation then **cross-checks** that the amount equals |Δbalance| on every row, so
97→    a misread or a dropped row still fails (it is *not* circular). Needs the printed opening balance
98→    to fix the first row's direction; returns ``[]`` if it can't form a clean series.
99→    """
100→    patterns = profile.date_patterns or (re.compile(r"^\d{2}[-/]\d{2}[-/]\d{4}$"),)
101→    rows = [[(str(c).strip() if c else "") for c in r] for r in (matrix or []) if r]
102→    if not rows or opening is None:
103→        return []
104→    ncols = max(len(r) for r in rows)
105→
106→    def is_date(s: str) -> bool:
107→        return any(p.match(s) for p in patterns)
108→
109→    def money(s: str) -> float | None:
110→        return parse_amount(s) if _MONEY_TOKEN.match(s) else None
111→
112→    # date column = the leftmost of the (first few) columns with the most date cells
113→    date_counts: dict[int, int] = {}
114→    for r in rows:
115→        for c in range(min(ncols, 3)):
116→            if c < len(r) and is_date(r[c]):
117→                date_counts[c] = date_counts.get(c, 0) + 1
118→    if not date_counts:
119→        return []
120→    top = max(date_counts.values())
121→    date_col = min(c for c, n in date_counts.items() if n == top)
122→
123→    txn_rows = [r for r in rows if date_col < len(r) and is_date(r[date_col])]
124→    if len(txn_rows) < 2:
125→        return []
126→
127→    # A money column's money cells must outnumber its date cells — so a sparsely-filled deposit
128→    # column still counts, but an interleaved value-date column (mostly dates) does not. The
129→    # running balance is the rightmost money column; the rest are the flow (withdrawal/deposit).
130→    money_cols = []
131→    for c in range(ncols):
132→        m = sum(1 for r in txn_rows if c < len(r) and money(r[c]) is not None)
133→        d = sum(1 for r in txn_rows if c < len(r) and is_date(r[c]))
134→        if m >= 1 and m > d:
135→            money_cols.append(c)
136→    if len(money_cols) < 2:
137→        return []
138→    balance_col = max(money_cols)
139→    flow_cols = [c for c in money_cols if c != balance_col]
140→
141→    text_cols = [c for c in range(ncols) if c != date_col and c not in money_cols]
142→    narr_col = (
143→        max(text_cols, key=lambda c: sum(len(r[c]) for r in txn_rows if c < len(r)))
144→        if text_cols
145→        else None
146→    )
147→
148→    out: list[ConceptRow] = []
149→    prev = opening
150→    for r in txn_rows:
151→        bal = money(r[balance_col]) if balance_col < len(r) else None
152→        if bal is None:
153→            return []  # a running balance on every row is required for the delta
154→        amt = next((money(r[c]) for c in flow_cols if c < len(r) and money(r[c]) is not None), None)
155→        debit = credit = None
156→        if amt is not None:
157→            if bal < prev - tol:
158→                debit = amt
159→            elif bal > prev + tol:
160→                credit = amt
161→            else:
162→                debit = amt  # zero net with an amount present → reconciliation will flag it
163→        out.append(
164→            ConceptRow(
165→                txn_date=r[date_col],
166→                narration=(r[narr_col] if narr_col is not None and narr_col < len(r) else ""),
167→                debit=debit,
168→                credit=credit,
169→                balance=bal,
170→            )
171→        )
172→        prev = bal
173→    return out
174→
175→
176→def parse_savings(pdf_path: Path, profile: Profile, password: str | None = None) -> SavingsParse:
177→    """Parse a savings statement into concept rows + a reconciliation verdict, per the profile.
178→
179→    Per-table mapping first (each extracted table mapped on its own; oracle-endorsed rows combined
180→    and re-checked), then a coordinate-clustering fallback. Returns ``verified=False`` if neither
181→    reconciles, so the caller can refuse to persist.
182→    """
183→    with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path, password=password) as pdf:
184→        words_by_page = [
185→            page.extract_words(use_text_flow=False, keep_blank_chars=False) for page in pdf.pages
186→        ]
187→        tables = [t for page in pdf.pages for t in (page.extract_tables() or [])]
188→        head = "\n".join((pdf.pages[i].extract_text() or "") for i in range(min(3, len(pdf.pages))))
189→
190→    account_number = profile.first_account(head) or "UNKNOWN"
191→    summary = parse_statement_summary(head)
192→    oracle = reconcile_oracle(summary.opening_balance, summary.closing_balance)
193→
194→    confirmed: list[ConceptRow] = []
195→    for table in tables:
196→        res = map_table(table, profile, oracle=oracle)
197→        if res and res.verified:
198→            confirmed.extend(res.rows)
199→    if confirmed and oracle(confirmed):
200→        return SavingsParse(account_number, confirmed, True)
201→
202→    matrix = cluster_to_matrix(words_by_page, keep_line=txn_line_predicate(profile))
203→    clustered = map_table(matrix, profile, oracle=oracle)
204→    if clustered and clustered.verified:
205→        return SavingsParse(account_number, clustered.rows, True)
206→
207→    # 3) balance-delta reconstruction — recovers vintages whose columns don't split into a clean
208→    #    debit/credit (merged amount column, interleaved value-date, sliver columns): derive the
209→    #    direction from the running balance and let the oracle cross-check amount == |Δbalance|.
210→    delta = _reconstruct_by_balance_delta(matrix, profile, summary.opening_balance)
211→    if delta and oracle(delta):
212→        return SavingsParse(account_number, delta, True)
213→
214→    fallback = confirmed or (clustered.rows if clustered else [])
215→    return SavingsParse(account_number, fallback, False)
216→
217→
218→__all__ = [
219→    "SavingsParse",
220→    "parse_savings",
221→    "parse_statement_summary",
222→    "txn_line_predicate",
223→    "reconcile_oracle",
224→]
225→

### 45. tool_result

1→"""Coordinate clustering — turn positioned words into a clean cell-matrix.
2→
3→When ``pdfplumber.extract_tables()`` mis-groups a semi-ruled or whitespace-aligned statement (the
4→Axis case: a date merged into the narration cell, column counts drifting between vintages), we read
5→**word positions** directly. Words are grouped into visual rows by their ``top``; column boundaries
6→are inferred from the vertical whitespace **shared across the transaction rows**; and each word is
7→dropped into the column its x-centre falls in. Because columns come from geometry, a leading date
8→sits in its *own* column instead of being glued to the narration.
9→
10→Two robustness lessons baked in:
11→
12→* **Infer columns from transaction-like rows only.** Headers, address blocks, and summaries span the
13→  page and would erase the inter-column whitespace. A caller-supplied ``keep_line`` predicate (e.g.
14→  "has a date *and* a money token") restricts both column inference and the emitted matrix to the
15→  transaction band.
16→* **Use a coverage histogram, not interval-merging.** A column boundary is an x-channel empty
17→  in *most* rows; a single long narration can't collapse two columns.
18→
19→Bank-agnostic extraction (no issuer constants, no x-positions hard-coded); the engine's oracle still
20→decides whether the result is trustworthy. Pure geometry in, strings out — no value is logged.
21→"""
22→
23→from __future__ import annotations
24→
25→from collections.abc import Callable, Sequence
26→
27→Word = dict  # a pdfplumber word: {"text", "x0", "x1", "top", "bottom", ...}
28→
29→
30→def group_lines(words: Sequence[Word], tol: float = 2.5) -> list[list[Word]]:
31→    """Group words into visual lines by ``top`` (tolerant of sub-pixel jitter), left-to-right."""
32→    lines: list[list[Word]] = []
33→    anchor_top: float | None = None
34→    for w in sorted(words, key=lambda w: (w["top"], w["x0"])):
35→        if lines and anchor_top is not None and abs(w["top"] - anchor_top) <= tol:
36→            lines[-1].append(w)
37→        else:
38→            lines.append([w])
39→            anchor_top = w["top"]
40→    return [sorted(line, key=lambda w: w["x0"]) for line in lines]
41→
42→
43→def infer_columns(
44→    lines: Sequence[Sequence[Word]], bin_w: float = 1.0, min_frac: float = 0.3
45→) -> list[tuple[float, float]]:
46→    """Infer column x-extents from a coverage histogram over ``lines``.
47→
48→    An x-bin is "filled" if at least ``min_frac`` of the lines have a word covering it; columns are
49→    the runs of filled bins, and the empty runs between them are the inter-column whitespace. Robust
50→    to a few full-width lines: it thresholds on the *fraction* of rows, not on any single one.
51→    """
52→    words = [w for line in lines for w in line]
53→    if not words:
54→        return []
55→    x0 = min(float(w["x0"]) for w in words)
56→    x1 = max(float(w["x1"]) for w in words)
57→    nbins = max(1, int((x1 - x0) / bin_w) + 1)
58→    cover = [0] * nbins
59→    for line in lines:
60→        marked: set[int] = set()
61→        for w in line:
62→            a = int((float(w["x0"]) - x0) / bin_w)
63→            b = int((float(w["x1"]) - x0) / bin_w)
64→            marked.update(range(max(0, a), min(nbins - 1, b) + 1))
65→        for k in marked:
66→            cover[k] += 1
67→    thresh = max(1.0, min_frac * len(lines))
68→    filled = [c >= thresh for c in cover]
69→    cols: list[tuple[float, float]] = []
70→    start: int | None = None
71→    for i, f in enumerate(filled):
72→        if f and start is None:
73→            start = i
74→        elif not f and start is not None:
75→            cols.append((x0 + start * bin_w, x0 + i * bin_w))
76→            start = None
77→    if start is not None:
78→        cols.append((x0 + start * bin_w, x0 + nbins * bin_w))
79→    return cols
80→
81→
82→def _column_of(center: float, columns: list[tuple[float, float]]) -> int:
83→    for i, (a, b) in enumerate(columns):
84→        if a <= center <= b:
85→            return i
86→    return min(
87→        range(len(columns)),
88→        key=lambda i: min(abs(center - columns[i][0]), abs(center - columns[i][1])),
89→    )
90→
91→
92→def line_to_cells(line: Sequence[Word], columns: list[tuple[float, float]]) -> list[str]:
93→    """Place each word into its column; join words that share a column (in x order)."""
94→    cells = ["" for _ in columns]
95→    for w in line:
96→        idx = _column_of((float(w["x0"]) + float(w["x1"])) / 2, columns)
97→        cells[idx] = (cells[idx] + " " + str(w["text"])).strip()
98→    return cells
99→
100→
101→def cluster_to_matrix(
102→    words_by_page: Sequence[Sequence[Word]],
103→    *,
104→    line_tol: float = 2.5,
105→    min_frac: float = 0.3,
106→    keep_line: Callable[[list[str]], bool] | None = None,
107→) -> list[list[str]]:
108→    """Cluster words across pages into one cell-matrix with consistent columns.
109→
110→    ``keep_line`` (texts of a line → bool) restricts both column inference and the output to the
111→    transaction band — without it, full-width headers/footers swamp the column structure. Columns
112→    are inferred once across all kept lines (a statement keeps its layout); rows are emitted in
113→    page-then-top order.
114→    """
115→    lines: list[list[Word]] = []
116→    for page in words_by_page:
117→        lines.extend(group_lines(page, line_tol))
118→    if keep_line is not None:
119→        lines = [ln for ln in lines if keep_line([str(w["text"]) for w in ln])]
120→    if not lines:
121→        return []
122→    columns = infer_columns(lines, min_frac=min_frac)
123→    if not columns:
124→        return []
125→    return [line_to_cells(line, columns) for line in lines]
126→
127→
128→__all__ = ["group_lines", "infer_columns", "line_to_cells", "cluster_to_matrix"]
129→

### 46. tool_result

1→"""Per-issuer profiles — bank knowledge expressed as **data, not code** (architecture §6).
2→
3→A profile carries the priors the generic engine needs to disambiguate one issuer: extra
4→concept↔label aliases, the accepted date shape, a positional fallback when there's no header,
5→table anchors, account-number patterns, quirks, and (parked) the password rule. Adding an issuer is
6→ideally *adding a profile*, not writing a parser.
7→
8→`HDFC_PROFILE` is data consumed by HDFC's parser, whose *extraction* stays bespoke coordinate-
9→clustering — the documented exception where `extract_tables()` fails on a semi-ruled table (see the
10→`hdfc-parser-tech-decision` memory). The Axis and AU Bank profiles land with their engine-driven
11→parsers (coordinate clustering → generic mapping → total reconcile).
12→"""
13→
14→from __future__ import annotations
15→
16→import re
17→
18→from .engine import Profile
19→
20→AXIS_PROFILE = Profile(
21→    name="axis",
22→    aliases={
23→        # Real Axis savings header: Tran/Txn Date | Transaction | Withdrawals | Deposits | Balance |
24→        # Other Information. "Transaction" is the narration column (not the date).
25→        "narration": ("transaction", "particulars"),
26→        "debit": ("withdrawals", "withdrawal", "debit"),
27→        "credit": ("deposits", "deposit", "credit"),
28→        "balance": ("balance",),
29→    },
30→    date_patterns=(re.compile(r"^\d{2}[-/]\d{2}[-/]\d{4}$"),),  # dd-mm-yyyy or dd/mm/yyyy
31→    # Layout / column-count varies between vintages, so there is NO reliable column_order — the
32→    # generic content search + total reconcile do the work. Broadened account pattern: the old
33→    # `Account No:` regex missed real statements.
34→    account_patterns=(
35→        re.compile(r"Account\s*(?:No|Number)\s*[:.\-]?\s*(\d{6,})", re.IGNORECASE),
36→        re.compile(r"A/c\s*No\.?\s*[:\s]*(\d{6,})", re.IGNORECASE),
37→    ),
38→    quirks=frozenset({"layout-varies", "other-information-column", "balance-optional"}),
39→)
40→
41→AUBANK_PROFILE = Profile(
42→    name="aubank",
43→    aliases={
44→        "narration": (
45→            "transaction",
46→            "particulars",
47→            "description",
48→            "narration",
49→            "transaction details",
50→        ),
51→        "debit": ("withdrawal", "withdrawals", "debit", "withdrawal amount", "dr"),
52→        "credit": ("deposit", "deposits", "credit", "deposit amount", "cr"),
53→        "balance": ("balance", "closing balance", "running balance"),
54→    },
55→    # AU vintages use dd-mm-yyyy / dd/mm/yyyy or dd-Mon-yyyy; verified on the real statements.
56→    date_patterns=(
57→        re.compile(r"^\d{2}[-/]\d{2}[-/]\d{4}$"),
58→        re.compile(r"^\d{2}-[A-Za-z]{3}-\d{4}$"),
59→    ),
60→    account_patterns=(
61→        re.compile(r"Account\s*(?:No|Number)\s*[:.\-]?\s*(\d{6,})", re.IGNORECASE),
62→        re.compile(r"A/c\s*No\.?\s*[:\s]*(\d{6,})", re.IGNORECASE),
63→    ),
64→    quirks=frozenset({"engine-driven"}),
65→)
66→
67→HDFC_PROFILE = Profile(
68→    name="hdfc",
69→    aliases={
70→        "debit": ("withdrawal", "withdrawals"),
71→        "credit": ("deposit", "deposits"),
72→        "balance": ("closing balance",),
73→    },
74→    date_patterns=(re.compile(r"^\d{2}/\d{2}/\d{4}$"),),  # dd/mm/yyyy
75→    table_anchors=("Narration", "Closing Balance"),
76→    account_patterns=(re.compile(r"Account\s*Number\s*[:.]?\s*(\d{6,})", re.IGNORECASE),),
77→    # HDFC's transaction table is semi-ruled; extract_tables() is unreliable on it, so HDFC extracts
78→    # by word-position clustering rather than through the generic cell-matrix engine.
79→    quirks=frozenset({"semi-ruled-table", "coordinate-clustering"}),
80→)
81→
82→__all__ = ["AXIS_PROFILE", "AUBANK_PROFILE", "HDFC_PROFILE"]
83→

### 47. tool_result

1→"""Deterministic promo-offer extraction from normalized email text.
2→
3→Precision-first: we anchor on an explicit "code" cue and validate the captured
4→token, so a promo email with *no* code (e.g. Flipkart's GOAT-sale banner)
5→correctly yields nothing rather than a hallucinated code. Codes rasterized into
6→images (e.g. Swiggy) are out of scope here — that's the OCR tier.
7→"""
8→
9→from __future__ import annotations
10→
11→import re
12→from dataclasses import dataclass
13→
14→_CODE_RE = re.compile(
15→    r"\b(?:coupon\s+code|promo\s*code|use\s+code|using\s+code|code)\b\s*[:\-]?\s*([A-Za-z0-9]{4,20})",
16→    re.IGNORECASE,
17→)
18→
19→_EXPIRY_PATTERNS: list[tuple[re.Pattern[str], str]] = [
20→    (re.compile(r"\bends today\b", re.I), "relative"),
21→    (re.compile(r"\blast few hours\b", re.I), "relative"),
22→    (re.compile(r"\bfinal day\b", re.I), "relative"),
23→    (re.compile(r"\bbefore\s+[A-Z][a-z]+\s+\d{1,2}\w*", re.I), "absolute"),
24→    (re.compile(r"\bvalid\s+(?:till|until|through)\b[^\n]{0,40}", re.I), "absolute"),
25→    (re.compile(r"\bexpir\w*\b[^\n]{0,40}", re.I), "absolute"),
26→]
27→
28→
29→def _looks_like_code(token: str) -> bool:
30→    """A code-like token has a digit, or is all-caps and reasonably long."""
31→    if any(ch.isdigit() for ch in token):
32→        return True
33→    return token.isupper() and len(token) >= 5
34→
35→
36→@dataclass
37→class OfferFields:
38→    codes: list[str]
39→    expiry_raw: str | None
40→    expiry_type: str | None
41→
42→
43→def extract_offer(text: str) -> OfferFields:
44→    """Pull promo code(s) and a best-effort expiry phrase from email text."""
45→    codes: list[str] = []
46→    for match in _CODE_RE.finditer(text):
47→        token = match.group(1)
48→        if _looks_like_code(token) and token not in codes:
49→            codes.append(token)
50→
51→    expiry_raw: str | None = None
52→    expiry_type: str | None = None
53→    for pattern, etype in _EXPIRY_PATTERNS:
54→        found = pattern.search(text)
55→        if found:
56→            expiry_raw = found.group(0).strip()
57→            expiry_type = etype
58→            break
59→
60→    return OfferFields(codes=codes, expiry_raw=expiry_raw, expiry_type=expiry_type)
61→

### 48. tool_result

1→"""Local OCR for codes rasterized into banner images (the image tier).
2→
3→100% local and permissive — pypdfium2 (Apache/BSD) renders pages, Tesseract
4→(Apache-2.0) reads them. No paid vision API (see DESIGN.md § 5 rule 9).
5→
6→Grounded on the corpus: grayscale render at ~288 DPI (scale 4.0) is what reliably
7→reads stylized banner text. Note OCR is approximate — e.g. Swiggy's white-on-orange
8→boxed `CTSWIGGYUX` reads as `ICTSWIGGYUX` (a leading box-edge artifact). Callers
9→should treat OCR-derived codes as low-confidence ("verify"), and matching against
10→known codes is fuzzy, never exact (docs/TESTING.md § 6). A purpose-trained model
11→is the eventual upgrade for stylized codes.
12→"""
13→
14→from __future__ import annotations
15→
16→import os
17→import shutil
18→import unicodedata
19→from pathlib import Path
20→
21→import pypdfium2 as pdfium
22→import pytesseract
23→from PIL import ImageOps
24→
25→# Common install locations, tried only if Tesseract isn't on PATH.
26→_DEFAULT_TESSERACT_PATHS = (
27→    r"C:\Program Files\Tesseract-OCR\tesseract.exe",  # Windows (winget / UB-Mannheim)
28→    "/usr/bin/tesseract",  # Linux (apt / dnf)
29→    "/usr/local/bin/tesseract",  # Linux from source / Intel Homebrew
30→    "/opt/homebrew/bin/tesseract",  # macOS Apple-silicon Homebrew
31→)
32→
33→
34→def _resolve_tesseract() -> str | None:
35→    """Find the Tesseract binary: env override, then PATH, then common OS locations."""
36→    cmd = os.environ.get("MUNEEM_TESSERACT_CMD")
37→    if cmd and Path(cmd).exists():
38→        return cmd
39→    found = shutil.which("tesseract")
40→    if found:
41→        return found
42→    for candidate in _DEFAULT_TESSERACT_PATHS:
43→        if Path(candidate).exists():
44→            return candidate
45→    return None
46→
47→
48→def tesseract_available() -> bool:
49→    return _resolve_tesseract() is not None
50→
51→
52→def ocr_pdf(pdf_path: Path | str, scale: float = 4.0) -> str:
53→    """Render each page locally and OCR it; returns NFKC-normalized text."""
54→    cmd = _resolve_tesseract()
55→    if cmd is None:
56→        raise RuntimeError(
57→            "Tesseract not found. Install it, or set MUNEEM_TESSERACT_CMD to its path."
58→        )
59→    pytesseract.pytesseract.tesseract_cmd = cmd
60→    pdf = pdfium.PdfDocument(str(pdf_path))
61→    parts: list[str] = []
62→    try:
63→        for page in pdf:
64→            image = ImageOps.grayscale(page.render(scale=scale).to_pil())
65→            parts.append(pytesseract.image_to_string(image))
66→    finally:
67→        pdf.close()
68→    return unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", "\n".join(parts))
69→

1→from __future__ import annotations
2→
3→from dataclasses import dataclass
4→from pathlib import Path
5→from typing import Protocol
6→
7→
8→@dataclass
9→class SearchCriteria:
10→    sender: str | None
11→    has_attachment: bool
12→    newer_than_days: int | None
13→
14→
15→class EmailProvider(Protocol):
16→    def authorize(self) -> None:
17→        """Authenticate and authorize with the provider."""
18→        ...
19→
20→    def search_messages(self, criteria: SearchCriteria, max_results: int = 100) -> list[str]:
21→        """Return a list of message IDs matching the search criteria."""
22→        ...
23→
24→    def get_headers(self, message_id: str) -> dict[str, str]:
25→        """Return a dictionary of headers (keys lower-cased) for the message."""
26→        ...
27→
28→    def list_pdf_attachments(self, message_id: str) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:
29→        """Return a list of (filename, attachment_id) for the message."""
30→        ...
31→
32→    def download_attachment(self, message_id: str, attachment_id: str, dest: Path) -> Path:
33→        """Download the attachment to the specified path and return the path."""
34→        ...

### 51. tool_result

1→from __future__ import annotations
2→
3→import base64
4→import json
5→from pathlib import Path
6→
7→from .base import EmailProvider, SearchCriteria
8→
9→SCOPES = ["https://www.googleapis.com/auth/gmail.readonly"]
10→_TOKEN_KEY = "gmail_token"
11→
12→
13→class GmailProvider(EmailProvider):
14→    def __init__(self, credentials_path: Path | str, open_browser: bool = True):
15→        self.credentials_path = credentials_path
16→        self.open_browser = open_browser
17→        self.service = None
18→
19→    def authorize(self) -> None:
20→        from google.auth.transport.requests import Request
21→        from google.oauth2.credentials import Credentials
22→        from google_auth_oauthlib.flow import InstalledAppFlow
23→        from googleapiclient.discovery import build
24→
25→        from muneem import secrets
26→
27→        creds = None
28→        raw = secrets.get_secret(_TOKEN_KEY)
29→        if raw:
30→            creds = Credentials.from_authorized_user_info(json.loads(raw), SCOPES)
31→        if creds and creds.valid:
32→            pass
33→        elif creds and creds.expired and creds.refresh_token:
34→            creds.refresh(Request())
35→        else:
36→            flow = InstalledAppFlow.from_client_secrets_file(str(self.credentials_path), SCOPES)
37→            creds = flow.run_local_server(port=0, open_browser=self.open_browser)
38→        secrets.set_secret(_TOKEN_KEY, creds.to_json())
39→        self.service = build("gmail", "v1", credentials=creds, cache_discovery=False)
40→
41→    def _build_query(self, criteria: SearchCriteria) -> str:
42→        parts = []
43→        if criteria.sender:
44→            parts.append(f"from:{criteria.sender}")
45→        if criteria.has_attachment:
46→            parts.append("has:attachment")
47→        if criteria.newer_than_days:
48→            parts.append(f"newer_than:{criteria.newer_than_days}d")
49→        return " ".join(parts)
50→
51→    def search_messages(self, criteria: SearchCriteria, max_results: int = 100) -> list[str]:
52→        if not self.service:
53→            self.authorize()
54→
55→        query = self._build_query(criteria)
56→        ids: list[str] = []
57→        page_token = None
58→        while True:
59→            resp = (
60→                self.service.users()
61→                .messages()
62→                .list(userId="me", q=query, pageToken=page_token, maxResults=min(max_results, 100))
63→                .execute()
64→            )
65→            ids.extend(m["id"] for m in resp.get("messages", []))
66→            page_token = resp.get("nextPageToken")
67→            if not page_token or len(ids) >= max_results:
68→                break
69→        return ids[:max_results]
70→
71→    def _get_message(self, message_id: str) -> dict:
72→        if not self.service:
73→            self.authorize()
74→        return (
75→            self.service.users().messages().get(userId="me", id=message_id, format="full").execute()
76→        )
77→
78→    def get_headers(self, message_id: str) -> dict[str, str]:
79→        message = self._get_message(message_id)
80→        return {
81→            h.get("name", "").lower(): h.get("value", "")
82→            for h in message.get("payload", {}).get("headers", [])
83→        }
84→
85→    def _walk_parts(self, payload: dict):
86→        stack = [payload]
87→        while stack:
88→            part = stack.pop()
89→            yield part
90→            stack.extend(part.get("parts", []))
91→
92→    def list_pdf_attachments(self, message_id: str) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:
93→        message = self._get_message(message_id)
94→        out: list[tuple[str, str]] = []
95→        for part in self._walk_parts(message.get("payload", {})):
96→            filename = part.get("filename") or ""
97→            att_id = part.get("body", {}).get("attachmentId")
98→            if att_id and filename.lower().endswith(".pdf"):
99→                out.append((filename, att_id))
100→        return out
101→
102→    def download_attachment(self, message_id: str, attachment_id: str, dest: Path) -> Path:
103→        if not self.service:
104→            self.authorize()
105→        att = (
106→            self.service.users()
107→            .messages()
108→            .attachments()
109→            .get(userId="me", messageId=message_id, id=attachment_id)
110→            .execute()
111→        )
112→        dest = Path(dest)
113→        dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
114→        dest.write_bytes(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(att["data"].encode()))
115→        return dest
116→

1→from __future__ import annotations
2→
3→import imaplib
4→from pathlib import Path
5→
6→from muneem import secrets
7→
8→from .base import EmailProvider, SearchCriteria
9→
10→_PASS_KEY = "imap_password"
11→
12→
13→class IMAPProvider(EmailProvider):
14→    def __init__(self, host: str, username: str):
15→        self.host = host
16→        self.username = username
17→        self.mail = None
18→
19→    def authorize(self) -> None:
20→        password = secrets.get_secret(f"{_PASS_KEY}_{self.username}")
21→        if not password:
22→            import getpass
23→
24→            password = getpass.getpass(f"Enter App Password for {self.username} at {self.host}: ")
25→            secrets.set_secret(f"{_PASS_KEY}_{self.username}", password)
26→
27→        self.mail = imaplib.IMAP4_SSL(self.host)
28→        self.mail.login(self.username, password)
29→        self.mail.select("inbox", readonly=True)
30→
31→    def _build_search(self, criteria: SearchCriteria) -> list[str]:
32→        search_args = []
33→        if criteria.sender:
34→            search_args.extend(["FROM", f'"{criteria.sender}"'])
35→        # IMAP doesn't reliably support native "has attachment" searching natively on all servers
36→        # We can fetch structure later to filter, but we omit it from standard search
37→        return search_args
38→
39→    def search_messages(self, criteria: SearchCriteria, max_results: int = 100) -> list[str]:
40→        if not self.mail:
41→            self.authorize()
42→
43→        search_args = self._build_search(criteria)
44→        if not search_args:
45→            search_args = ["ALL"]
46→
47→        status, messages = self.mail.search(None, *search_args)
48→        if status != "OK":
49→            raise RuntimeError("IMAP search failed")
50→
51→        msg_ids = messages[0].split()
52→        return [mid.decode() for mid in msg_ids[-max_results:]]
53→
54→    def get_headers(self, message_id: str) -> dict[str, str]:
55→        raise NotImplementedError(
56→            "IMAPProvider.get_headers is not yet implemented. "
57→            "Attachment download and header normalization are pending."
58→        )
59→
60→    def list_pdf_attachments(self, message_id: str) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:
61→        raise NotImplementedError(
62→            "IMAPProvider.list_pdf_attachments is not yet implemented. "
63→            "Attachment download and header normalization are pending."
64→        )
65→
66→    def download_attachment(self, message_id: str, attachment_id: str, dest_dir: Path) -> Path:
67→        raise NotImplementedError(
68→            "IMAPProvider.download_attachment is not yet implemented. "
69→            "Attachment download and header normalization are pending."
70→        )
71→

### 53. tool_result

1→from __future__ import annotations
2→
3→import json
4→from pathlib import Path
5→
6→from .base import EmailProvider, SearchCriteria
7→
8→_TOKEN_KEY = "outlook_token"
9→SCOPES = ["Mail.Read"]
10→
11→
12→class OutlookProvider(EmailProvider):
13→    def __init__(self, client_id: str, authority: str = "https://login.microsoftonline.com/common"):
14→        self.client_id = client_id
15→        self.authority = authority
16→        self.access_token = None
17→
18→    def authorize(self) -> None:
19→        try:
20→            from msal import PublicClientApplication
21→        except ImportError:
22→            msg = "msal is required for OutlookProvider. Run pip install msal"
23→            raise RuntimeError(msg) from None
24→
25→        from muneem import secrets
26→
27→        app = PublicClientApplication(self.client_id, authority=self.authority)
28→
29→        # Try to find a cached token (simplified)
30→        raw = secrets.get_secret(_TOKEN_KEY)
31→        if raw:
32→            token_data = json.loads(raw)
33→            self.access_token = token_data.get("access_token")
34→            # Ideally, handle refresh logic here via msal TokenCache
35→            return
36→
37→        # Interactive login
38→        flow = app.initiate_device_flow(scopes=SCOPES)
39→        if "user_code" not in flow:
40→            raise ValueError("Failed to create device flow")
41→
42→        print(flow["message"])  # Instructs user to go to a URL and enter code
43→        result = app.acquire_token_by_device_flow(flow)
44→
45→        if "access_token" in result:
46→            self.access_token = result["access_token"]
47→            secrets.set_secret(_TOKEN_KEY, json.dumps({"access_token": self.access_token}))
48→        else:
49→            raise RuntimeError(f"Failed to authenticate: {result.get('error_description')}")
50→
51→    def _build_filter(self, criteria: SearchCriteria) -> str:
52→        parts = []
53→        if criteria.sender:
54→            parts.append(f"from/emailAddress/address eq '{criteria.sender}'")
55→        if criteria.has_attachment:
56→            parts.append("hasAttachments eq true")
57→        if criteria.newer_than_days:
58→            pass  # Graph API requires actual datetime subtraction for receivedDateTime
59→
60→        return " and ".join(parts)
61→
62→    def search_messages(self, criteria: SearchCriteria, max_results: int = 100) -> list[str]:
63→        if not self.access_token:
64→            self.authorize()
65→
66→        try:
67→            import requests
68→        except ImportError:
69→            raise RuntimeError(
70→                "requests is required for OutlookProvider. Run: pip install requests msal"
71→            ) from None
72→
73→        url = "https://graph.microsoft.com/v1.0/me/messages"
74→        headers = {"Authorization": f"Bearer {self.access_token}"}
75→        params: dict = {"$select": "id"}
76→
77→        filter_str = self._build_filter(criteria)
78→        if filter_str:
79→            params["$filter"] = filter_str
80→
81→        params["$top"] = min(max_results, 100)
82→
83→        resp = requests.get(url, headers=headers, params=params)
84→        resp.raise_for_status()
85→
86→        data = resp.json()
87→        return [msg["id"] for msg in data.get("value", [])]
88→
89→    def get_headers(self, message_id: str) -> dict[str, str]:
90→        raise NotImplementedError(
91→            "OutlookProvider.get_headers is not yet implemented. "
92→            "This provider is experimental. Only basic search works today."
93→        )
94→
95→    def list_pdf_attachments(self, message_id: str) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:
96→        raise NotImplementedError(
97→            "OutlookProvider.list_pdf_attachments is not yet implemented. "
98→            "This provider is experimental. Only basic search works today."
99→        )
100→
101→    def download_attachment(self, message_id: str, attachment_id: str, dest_dir: Path) -> Path:
102→        raise NotImplementedError(
103→            "OutlookProvider.download_attachment is not yet implemented. "
104→            "This provider is experimental. Only basic search works today."
105→        )

### 55. tool_result

1→#!/bin/sh
2→# muneem pre-commit guard — blocks staging of sensitive files (real statements,
3→# secrets, local DBs). See .githooks/precommit_check.py for the rules.
4→# Locates a Python interpreter so this works on Linux/WSL and Git-for-Windows.
5→# Enable with:  git config core.hooksPath .githooks   (or `make hooks`).
6→dir=$(dirname "$0")
7→for py in python3 python py; do
8→    if command -v "$py" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
9→        exec "$py" "$dir/precommit_check.py"
10→    fi
11→done
12→echo "muneem pre-commit: no python interpreter found; skipping the sensitive-file guard." >&2
13→echo "  (install python to enable; tests/test_repo_hygiene.py is the post-commit backstop)" >&2
14→exit 0
15→

### 56. tool_result

1→#!/bin/sh
2→# muneem pre-push gate — runs lint + the offline test suite (including the DB invariant
3→# tripwires in tests/test_db_invariants.py) so only green code reaches GitHub. This is the
4→# LOCAL substitute for server-side branch protection, which GitHub gates behind Pro / public
5→# repos (this repo is private by design).
6→#
7→# Bypass intentionally (the "explicitly allowed" escape hatch) with:
8→#     git push --no-verify
9→#
10→# Enable with:  git config core.hooksPath .githooks   (or `make hooks`).
11→
12→root=$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null) || exit 0
13→
14→# Prefer the project venv (it has ruff + pytest); fall back to a PATH python.
15→if [ -x "$root/.venv/bin/python" ]; then
16→    py="$root/.venv/bin/python"
17→elif [ -x "$root/.venv/Scripts/python.exe" ]; then
18→    py="$root/.venv/Scripts/python.exe"
19→else
20→    py=""
21→    for cand in python3 python py; do
22→        if command -v "$cand" >/dev/null 2>&1; then py="$cand"; break; fi
23→    done
24→fi
25→
26→if [ -z "$py" ]; then
27→    echo "muneem pre-push: no python/venv found; skipping the lint+test gate (CI still runs)." >&2
28→    exit 0
29→fi
30→
31→# If the dev tooling isn't installed in this interpreter, skip rather than false-block a push.
32→if ! "$py" -m pytest --version >/dev/null 2>&1; then
33→    echo "muneem pre-push: pytest not available in $py; skipping gate (run 'make install')." >&2
34→    echo "  CI still runs on push; or override now with: git push --no-verify" >&2
35→    exit 0
36→fi
37→
38→fail() {
39→    echo "muneem pre-push: $1 — push BLOCKED." >&2
40→    echo "  Fix it, or bypass intentionally with: git push --no-verify" >&2
41→    exit 1
42→}
43→
44→echo "muneem pre-push: ruff check ..." >&2
45→"$py" -m ruff check "$root" || fail "ruff check failed"
46→echo "muneem pre-push: ruff format --check ..." >&2
47→"$py" -m ruff format --check "$root" || fail "ruff format check failed"
48→echo "muneem pre-push: pytest (offline suite) ..." >&2
49→"$py" -m pytest -q "$root" || fail "tests failed"
50→
51→echo "muneem pre-push: all checks passed." >&2
52→exit 0
53→

### 57. tool_result

1→#!/usr/bin/env python3
2→"""muneem sensitive-file guard (invoked by the pre-commit hook).
3→
4→Refuses to *stage* files that policy says must never be committed: real
5→statements (any PDF, or anything under ``testdata/`` outside the allowed
6→corpora), OAuth secrets, password rules, local databases, key material, and
7→``.env`` files. This is the PRE-commit backstop to ``.gitignore`` — it stops the
8→``git add -f`` / ``git add -A`` mistake before it becomes a commit (the very
9→thing that caused an earlier statement leak). The post-commit complement is
10→``tests/test_repo_hygiene.py``.
11→
12→Exit 0 when nothing sensitive is staged, 1 otherwise. A deliberate, reviewed
13→exception can be committed with ``git commit --no-verify``.
14→"""
15→
16→from __future__ import annotations
17→
18→import fnmatch
19→import os
20→import subprocess
21→import sys
22→
23→# Only these subtrees of testdata/ are non-sensitive and may be committed.
24→ALLOWED_PREFIXES = ("testdata/promo-emails/", "testdata/synthetic/")
25→SECRET_NAMES = {"credentials.json", "token.json", "token.pickle", "passwords.txt"}
26→
27→
28→def is_sensitive(path: str) -> str | None:
29→    """Return a human-readable reason if ``path`` must not be committed, else None."""
30→    p = path.replace("\\", "/").strip()
31→    if not p or p.startswith(ALLOWED_PREFIXES):
32→        return None
33→    name = p.rsplit("/", 1)[-1]
34→    low = p.lower()
35→    nlow = name.lower()
36→    if p.startswith("testdata/"):
37→        return "under testdata/ — sensitive by default (only promo-emails/ and synthetic/ allowed)"
38→    if low.endswith(".pdf"):
39→        return "PDF outside the allowed corpora (real statements must stay local)"
40→    if low.endswith((".db", ".sqlite", ".sqlite3")):
41→        return "local database (may contain parsed statement data)"
42→    if low.endswith(".plaintext.bak"):
43→        return "plaintext database backup (cleartext from 'muneem db encrypt' migration)"
44→    if low.endswith((".pem", ".key")):
45→        return "key material"
46→    if nlow in SECRET_NAMES:
47→        return "OAuth / credential secret"
48→    if nlow.startswith("oauth_token"):
49→        return "OAuth token"
50→    # Block the rules *data* file (password_rules.yaml/.yml/.json/…) but not the loader module
51→    # (src/muneem/password_rules.py is code, not a secret).
52→    if fnmatch.fnmatch(nlow, "password_rules.*") and not nlow.endswith(".py"):
53→        return "password rules"
54→    if (nlow == ".env" or nlow.startswith(".env.")) and nlow != ".env.example":
55→        return ".env file (may contain secrets)"
56→    return None
57→
58→
59→def _staged_paths() -> list[str]:
60→    # Tests inject a newline-separated list via PRECOMMIT_TEST_FILES (no git needed).
61→    override = os.environ.get("PRECOMMIT_TEST_FILES")
62→    if override is not None:
63→        return [ln for ln in override.replace("\r\n", "\n").split("\n") if ln.strip()]
64→    # -z keeps paths with spaces/unicode intact (the real statements have spaces).
65→    out = subprocess.run(
66→        ["git", "diff", "--cached", "--name-only", "-z", "--diff-filter=ACM"],
67→        capture_output=True,
68→        text=True,
69→        check=True,
70→    ).stdout
71→    return [p for p in out.split("\0") if p.strip()]
72→
73→
74→def main() -> int:
75→    offenders = [(p, reason) for p in _staged_paths() if (reason := is_sensitive(p))]
76→    if not offenders:
77→        return 0
78→    print("muneem pre-commit: refusing to commit sensitive file(s):", file=sys.stderr)
79→    for p, reason in offenders:
80→        print(f"  x {p}\n      {reason}", file=sys.stderr)
81→    print(
82→        "\nThese must never reach git (CLAUDE.md security rules; see .gitignore).\n"
83→        "If this is a deliberate, reviewed exception: git commit --no-verify",
84→        file=sys.stderr,
85→    )
86→    return 1
87→
88→
89→if __name__ == "__main__":
90→    raise SystemExit(main())
91→

1→name: ci
2→
3→on:
4→  # All-branch pushes so feature branches are gated even before a PR exists
5→  # (the regression fixtures in tests/test_regression_synth.py run here, secret-free).
6→  push:
7→  pull_request:
8→  workflow_dispatch:
9→
10→jobs:
11→  test:
12→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
13→    strategy:
14→      fail-fast: false
15→      matrix:
16→        python-version: ["3.11", "3.12", "3.13"]
17→    steps:
18→      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
19→      - uses: actions/setup-python@v6
20→        with:
21→          python-version: ${{ matrix.python-version }}
22→          cache: pip
23→          cache-dependency-path: pyproject.toml
24→      - name: Install
25→        run: |
26→          python -m pip install --upgrade pip
27→          python -m pip install -e '.[dev]'
28→      - name: Lint

32→      - name: Test

34→
35→  ocr:

48→      - name: Install

51→          python -m pip install -e '.[dev]'
52→      - name: OCR tests

### 59. tool_result

1→"""Security guard: no sensitive paths may be tracked in git.
2→
3→Encodes DESIGN.md section 5 ("Nothing sensitive ever in git") and docs/TESTING.md
4→section 9 as a failing-build assertion. If this test fails, something that must
5→stay local has been committed.
6→"""
7→
8→from __future__ import annotations
9→
10→import subprocess
11→from pathlib import Path
12→
13→REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
14→
15→FORBIDDEN_PREFIXES = ("testdata/statements/", "testdata/private/")
16→FORBIDDEN_SUFFIXES = (".db", ".sqlite", ".sqlite3", ".env", ".pem", ".key")
17→FORBIDDEN_NAMES = (
18→    "token.json",
19→    "token.pickle",
20→    "credentials.json",
21→    "password_rules.yaml",
22→    "password_rules.yml",
23→    "password_rules.json",
24→    "passwords.txt",
25→)
26→
27→
28→def _tracked_files() -> list[str]:
29→    out = subprocess.run(
30→        ["git", "ls-files"],
31→        cwd=REPO_ROOT,
32→        capture_output=True,
33→        text=True,
34→        check=True,
35→    )
36→    return [line.strip() for line in out.stdout.splitlines() if line.strip()]
37→
38→
39→def test_no_sensitive_paths_tracked():
40→    offenders = []
41→    for path in _tracked_files():
42→        low = path.lower()
43→        if (
44→            low.startswith(FORBIDDEN_PREFIXES)
45→            or low.endswith(FORBIDDEN_SUFFIXES)
46→            or Path(path).name.lower() in FORBIDDEN_NAMES
47→        ):
48→            offenders.append(path)
49→    assert not offenders, f"sensitive paths must never be tracked: {offenders}"
50→
51→
52→def test_only_corpus_pdfs_are_tracked():
53→    """Statements are PDFs — the only PDFs git may track are the non-sensitive promo corpus.
54→
55→    This is the guard that would have caught real HDFC statements being committed to
56→    testdata/hdfc-statements/.
57→    """
58→    offenders = [
59→        path
60→        for path in _tracked_files()
61→        if path.lower().endswith(".pdf") and not path.startswith("testdata/promo-emails/")
62→    ]
63→    assert not offenders, f"only testdata/promo-emails/ PDFs may be tracked; found: {offenders}"
64→
65→
66→def test_no_noncorpus_testdata_is_tracked():
67→    """Mirror of the pre-commit guard: under testdata/, only the non-sensitive
68→    promo-emails/ and synthetic/ corpora may be tracked — nothing else (real
69→    statements, zips, credentials, snapshots all stay local)."""
70→    allowed = ("testdata/promo-emails/", "testdata/synthetic/")
71→    offenders = [
72→        path
73→        for path in _tracked_files()
74→        if path.startswith("testdata/") and not path.startswith(allowed)
75→    ]
76→    assert not offenders, (
77→        f"only promo-emails/ and synthetic/ may be tracked under testdata/; found: {offenders}"
78→    )
79→

1→"""Rugged DB invariants — CI tripwires that block a PR when a *crucial* database guarantee
2→regresses, **unless the change is made explicit in the same PR**.
3→
4→Two kinds of guard:
5→
6→1. **Security / integrity invariants** that must ALWAYS hold — a failure is a real regression to
7→   fix, never something to "allow":
8→     - the production ledger is *never* written in plaintext (encryption seam not bypassed);
9→     - a keyed open never silently downgrades to plaintext if the SQLCipher binding is missing;
10→     - foreign keys are enforced (and cascade);
11→     - `documents.sha256` dedup is unique.
12→
13→2. **Schema lock** — pins ``SCHEMA_VERSION`` and the exact table/column shape. An *accidental*
14→   schema drift fails CI. A *deliberate* schema change is "explicitly allowed" by updating
15→   ``EXPECTED_SCHEMA`` / ``EXPECTED_SCHEMA_VERSION`` below **in the same PR** (a visible, reviewable
16→   diff) — ideally paired with a migration. This is the tripwire the request asks for: schema
17→   churn can't sneak through a merge unnoticed.
18→
19→All checks are offline, fast, and use fabricated data only.
20→"""
21→
22→from __future__ import annotations
23→
24→import sqlite3
25→import sys
26→
27→import pytest
28→
29→from muneem import db as dbmod
30→
31→# --- Schema lock -------------------------------------------------------------------------------
32→# Update BOTH of these (and add a migration) when you intentionally change the schema. That edit
33→# is the "explicitly allowed" signal — it shows up in the PR diff and forces a conscious decision.
34→
35→EXPECTED_SCHEMA_VERSION = 4
36→
37→# table -> [(column, declared_type, notnull, pk), ...] as reported by PRAGMA table_info.
38→EXPECTED_SCHEMA: dict[str, list[tuple[str, str, int, int]]] = {
39→    "au_transactions": [

1→import json
2→import os
3→from pathlib import Path
4→
5→import pytest
6→
7→
8→@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
9→def _isolate_keychain(monkeypatch):
10→    """Never touch the real OS keychain in tests.
11→
12→    Replaces :mod:`muneem.secrets` with an in-memory store so the suite cannot pollute the
13→    developer's keychain (e.g. minting a DB key) and runs cleanly in headless CI where no
14→    keyring backend exists. Tests that need specific keychain behaviour re-patch on top.
15→    """
16→    store: dict[str, str] = {}
17→
18→    def _set(name, value):
19→        store[name] = value
20→
21→    monkeypatch.setattr("muneem.secrets.get_secret", store.get)
22→    monkeypatch.setattr("muneem.secrets.set_secret", _set)
23→    monkeypatch.setattr("muneem.secrets.delete_secret", lambda name: store.pop(name, None))
24→    return store
25→
26→
27→@pytest.fixture
28→def snapshot():
29→    def _assert_snapshot(name: str, data: list | dict):
30→        snapshot_dir = Path("testdata/snapshots")
31→        snapshot_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
32→        snapshot_file = snapshot_dir / f"{name}.json"
33→
34→        if os.environ.get("UPDATE_SNAPSHOTS") == "1":
35→            with snapshot_file.open("w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
36→                json.dump(data, f, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)
37→            return True
38→
39→        if not snapshot_file.exists():
40→            pytest.fail(f"Snapshot not found at {snapshot_file}. Run with UPDATE_SNAPSHOTS=1.")
41→
42→        with snapshot_file.open("r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
43→            expected = json.load(f)
44→
45→        assert data == expected, f"Data does not match snapshot {name}"
46→        return True
47→
48→    return _assert_snapshot

### 62. tool_result

1→"""At-rest encryption for the local ledger (SQLCipher via sqlcipher3).
2→
3→Covers the safety-critical invariants of the encryption seam:
4→- a keyed open round-trips data through SQLCipher with SQL queries intact;
5→- a wrong key is rejected (never silently returns garbage / empty);
6→- the on-disk file is genuinely encrypted (stdlib sqlite3 cannot read it);
7→- ``:memory:`` and keyless opens stay plaintext (the test / CI path);
8→- the keychain-backed key is minted once and reused;
9→- a pre-existing *plaintext* ledger is refused by ``connect_encrypted`` and can be
10→  migrated in place by ``encrypt_database``;
11→- the key never appears in an error message.
12→
13→No real statement data is involved — only fabricated numbers.
14→"""
15→
16→from __future__ import annotations
17→
18→import sqlite3
19→
20→import pytest
21→
22→pytest.importorskip("sqlcipher3")  # encryption backend; a hard dep, skip only if truly absent
23→
24→from muneem import db as dbmod  # noqa: E402 — after importorskip by design
25→
26→KEY = "00112233445566778899aabbccddeeff00112233445566778899aabbccddeeff"
27→WRONG = "ffeeddccbbaa99887766554433221100ffeeddccbbaa99887766554433221100"
28→
29→
30→def _fake_keychain(monkeypatch):
31→    """Replace the OS keychain with an in-memory dict for deterministic, offline tests."""
32→    store: dict[str, str] = {}
33→
34→    def _set(name, value):
35→        store[name] = value
36→
37→    monkeypatch.setattr("muneem.secrets.get_secret", lambda name: store.get(name))
38→    monkeypatch.setattr("muneem.secrets.set_secret", _set)
39→    return store
40→
41→
42→def test_encrypted_roundtrip_keeps_sql_queryability(tmp_path):
43→    p = tmp_path / "led.db"
44→    with dbmod.connect(p, key=KEY) as conn:
45→        conn.execute("CREATE TABLE t (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, amount REAL, note TEXT)")
46→        conn.executemany(
47→            "INSERT INTO t (amount, note) VALUES (?, ?)", [(100.5, "a"), (250.0, "b"), (75.25, "c")]
48→        )
49→    # Reopen with the same key; WHERE / SUM / ORDER BY all work through the encryption.
50→    with dbmod.connect(p, key=KEY) as conn:
51→        total = conn.execute("SELECT SUM(amount) FROM t WHERE amount > 80").fetchone()[0]
52→        assert total == pytest.approx(350.5)
53→        rows = conn.execute("SELECT amount FROM t ORDER BY amount").fetchall()
54→        assert [r["amount"] for r in rows] == [75.25, 100.5, 250.0]
55→
56→
57→def test_wrong_key_is_rejected(tmp_path):
58→    p = tmp_path / "led.db"
59→    with dbmod.connect(p, key=KEY) as conn:
60→        conn.execute("CREATE TABLE t (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, x REAL)")
61→        conn.execute("INSERT INTO t (x) VALUES (1.0)")
62→    with pytest.raises(dbmod.EncryptedDatabaseError):
63→        with dbmod.connect(p, key=WRONG):
64→            pass  # the wrong key is caught at open (key verification), before any query
65→
66→
67→def test_on_disk_file_is_actually_encrypted(tmp_path):
68→    p = tmp_path / "led.db"
69→    with dbmod.connect(p, key=KEY) as conn:
70→        conn.execute("CREATE TABLE t (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, x REAL)")
71→        conn.execute("INSERT INTO t (x) VALUES (1.0)")
72→    # Not a plaintext SQLite file, and stock stdlib sqlite3 cannot open it.
73→    assert not dbmod.is_plaintext_sqlite(p)
74→    con = sqlite3.connect(p)
75→    try:
76→        with pytest.raises(sqlite3.DatabaseError):
77→            con.execute("SELECT * FROM t").fetchone()
78→    finally:
79→        con.close()
80→

3→Exercises the ladder on synthetic cell-matrices (no PDF/DB): L0 header lexicon, the profile-order
4→fallback, L1/L2 content search with the oracle picking the self-identifying balance column, and a
5→deliberately non-bank ``FamilySpec`` to prove the engine is family-agnostic.
6→"""

9→from dataclasses import dataclass

1→"""Regression over the committed promo corpus, driven by expected.yaml.

5→(unverified) are intentionally excluded here — see docs/TESTING.md sections 3-4.
6→"""

34→            assert code in got, f"{name}: expected {code!r} in {got}"

1→"""Enforce muneem's commercial-license principle (DESIGN.md § 5 rule 8).
2→
3→Every declared dependency must be on the permissive allowlist below (kept in sync
4→with docs/DEPENDENCIES.md). Adding a dependency without recording its
5→MIT/BSD/Apache/… license here fails the build, forcing a license review — and
6→keeps banned copyleft licenses (notably AGPL, e.g. PyMuPDF) out of the project.
7→"""
8→
9→from __future__ import annotations
10→
11→import re
12→import tomllib
13→from pathlib import Path
14→
15→REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
16→
17→# Normalized (lowercased) dependency name -> license. Permissive / commercial-safe only.
18→ALLOWLIST = {
19→    "typer": "MIT",
20→    "platformdirs": "MIT",
21→    "keyring": "MIT",
22→    "pdfminer.six": "MIT",
23→    "pypdfium2": "Apache-2.0/BSD-3-Clause",
24→    "pytesseract": "Apache-2.0",
25→    "pillow": "HPND",
26→    "pypdf": "BSD-3-Clause",
27→    "google-api-python-client": "Apache-2.0",
28→    "google-auth": "Apache-2.0",
29→    "google-auth-oauthlib": "Apache-2.0",
30→    "pdfplumber": "MIT",
31→    # SQLCipher at-rest encryption. Binding (sqlcipher3) = Zlib; bundled native deps are
32→    # SQLCipher core = BSD-3-Clause, SQLite = Public Domain, OpenSSL = Apache-2.0 — all
33→    # permissive (see docs/DEPENDENCIES.md).
34→    "sqlcipher3": "Zlib",
35→    "pytest": "MIT",
36→    "ruff": "MIT",
37→    "pyyaml": "MIT",
38→    "msal": "MIT",
39→    "requests": "Apache-2.0",
40→}
41→
42→BANNED_SUBSTRINGS = ("GPL", "AGPL", "AFFERO")
43→
44→
45→def _declared_dependency_names() -> set[str]:
46→    data = tomllib.loads((REPO_ROOT / "pyproject.toml").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
47→    specs = list(data["project"].get("dependencies", []))
48→    for extra in data["project"].get("optional-dependencies", {}).values():
49→        specs.extend(extra)
50→    names = set()
51→    for spec in specs:
52→        # "pdfminer.six>=20240706" / "typer[all]>=0.12" -> bare name
53→        name = re.split(r"[<>=!~;\[ ]", spec, maxsplit=1)[0].strip().lower()
54→        if name:
55→            names.add(name)
56→    return names
57→
58→
59→def test_every_dependency_is_allowlisted():
60→    unknown = sorted(_declared_dependency_names() - set(ALLOWLIST))
61→    assert not unknown, (
62→        f"dependencies not on the commercial-safe allowlist: {unknown}. "
63→        "Add them to docs/DEPENDENCIES.md and ALLOWLIST with their license, or remove them."
64→    )
65→
66→
67→def test_allowlist_contains_no_banned_licenses():
68→    bad = {
69→        name: lic
70→        for name, lic in ALLOWLIST.items()
71→        if any(token in lic.upper() for token in BANNED_SUBSTRINGS)
72→    }
73→    assert not bad, f"copyleft licenses are banned but present in allowlist: {bad}"
74→

1→"""Dependency-free, born-digital PDF generator for regression fixtures (stdlib only).
2→
3→Layer 1 of the regression-CI plan (ROADMAP § "Planned — Regression CI") needs **full-pipeline**
4→fixtures — real PDFs that go through pdfminer/pdfplumber, not synthetic word-lists that bypass them.
5→Rather than add a dependency (reportlab etc.), we emit a minimal valid PDF with **positioned
6→Helvetica text** (`Tm`/`Tj`), so it stays commercial-safe and CI-portable.
7→
8→Coordinates: callers think in pdfplumber's top-down `top` (distance from the page top); we convert
9→to PDF's bottom-up baseline. Absolute `top` need not be exact — parsers key on x-positions, same-row
10→grouping, and row order — but words on one row share a baseline (one `top`) and rows are separated.
11→Amount columns are **right-aligned** via real Helvetica metrics so their right edges (`x1`) land in
12→the bands the HDFC clusterer / engine expect.
13→"""

1→import os

7→from muneem import unlock

9→
10→def _write_pdf(path: Path, password: str | None = None) -> Path:
11→    writer = pypdf.PdfWriter()
12→    writer.add_blank_page(width=200, height=200)
13→    if password:
14→        writer.encrypt(password)
15→    with open(path, "wb") as fh:
16→        writer.write(fh)
17→    return path
18→
19→
20→@pytest.fixture
21→def encrypted_pdf(tmp_path):
22→    return _write_pdf(tmp_path / "enc.pdf", password="secret123")
23→
24→
25→@pytest.fixture
26→def plain_pdf(tmp_path):
27→    return _write_pdf(tmp_path / "plain.pdf")
28→
29→
30→def test_is_encrypted(encrypted_pdf, plain_pdf):
31→    assert unlock.is_encrypted(encrypted_pdf)
32→    assert not unlock.is_encrypted(plain_pdf)
33→
34→
35→def test_unlock_via_candidate(encrypted_pdf, tmp_path):
36→    res = unlock.unlock(encrypted_pdf, candidates=["wrong", "secret123"], dest=tmp_path / "out.pdf")
37→    assert res.ok and res.method == "candidate" and res.password == "secret123"
38→    assert res.output_path.exists()
39→    assert not unlock.is_encrypted(res.output_path)  # the decrypted copy opens without a password

111→def _schema_fingerprint(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> dict[str, list[tuple[str, str, int, int]]]:
112→    tables = [
113→        r[0]
114→        for r in conn.execute(
115→            "SELECT name FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table' AND name NOT LIKE 'sqlite_%' "
116→            "ORDER BY name"
117→        )
118→    ]
119→    out: dict[str, list[tuple[str, str, int, int]]] = {}
120→    for t in tables:
121→        cols = conn.execute(f"PRAGMA table_info({t})").fetchall()
122→        # (cid, name, type, notnull, dflt_value, pk) -> (name, TYPE, notnull, pk)
123→        out[t] = [(c[1], (c[2] or "").upper(), int(c[3]), int(c[5])) for c in cols]
124→    return out
125→
126→

141→    if actual != EXPECTED_SCHEMA:
142→        diffs: list[str] = []
143→        for t in sorted(set(EXPECTED_SCHEMA) | set(actual)):
144→            if t not in actual:
145→                diffs.append(f"  - table REMOVED: {t}")
146→            elif t not in EXPECTED_SCHEMA:
147→                diffs.append(f"  - table ADDED: {t} -> {actual[t]}")
148→            elif actual[t] != EXPECTED_SCHEMA[t]:
149→                diffs.append(
150→                    f"  - table CHANGED: {t}\n      expected {EXPECTED_SCHEMA[t]}\n"
151→                    f"      actual   {actual[t]}"
152→                )
153→        raise AssertionError(
154→            "DB schema lock tripped — the on-disk shape changed:\n"
155→            + "\n".join(diffs)
156→            + "\n\nIf this change is INTENTIONAL: update EXPECTED_SCHEMA (and bump "
157→            "EXPECTED_SCHEMA_VERSION + add a migration) in this same PR. That edit is the "
158→            "explicit allow. If it is NOT intentional, you just caught a regression."
159→        )
